## PART 5 — THE PRESS SYSTEM: FROM PIGEONS TO PRINT

### 5.1 The documented instruments

- **Salomon and the *Allgemeine Zeitung*:** pressure exerted through Gentz — Metternich's own publicist, whom the family cultivated; the AZ's coverage of the house softened accordingly **[S: Ferguson]**. (The retainer amount circulated in the literature remains **unverified** — Anomaly #1.)
- **Heine as retained correspondent:** the poet-journalist's coverage of James was "**relatively positive (if satirical)**" — documented relationship, exact terms unverified **[S: Ferguson]**.
- **James and the Paris papers:** documented influence over the *Moniteur Universel* and the *Journal des débats*, with the correspondence preserved.

### 5.2 The operator's voice (verbatim)

- **James, 1837:** "**I am arranging for several articles to be printed in the newspapers for this will make an impression in Madrid and in London because your English newspapers often follow in the footsteps of our French ones and it is good if one can regulate public opinion**" **[S: Ferguson, letter]**.
- **James, 1839:** he would "**see to it**" that the French government was "**attacked in all the newspapers**" if it opposed his railway plans — "**Newspapers can make a strong impact**" **[S: Ferguson, letter]**.
- The doctrine behind both, from Mayer Amschel's maxims: "**If you can't make yourself loved, make yourself feared**" — and the capture principle beneath the whole gift-and-fee system of Part 3.1: "**If a high-placed person enters into a [financial] partnership with a Jew, he belongs to the Jew**" **[B: Ferguson]**.
- The starting conditions, documented: the 1820s press was *hostile* — the family were press targets before they became press managers; the system's later reputation for controlling opinion was earned, not inherited **[S]**.

### 5.3 The correction: the challengers did it too

The Pereire war's documentation shows the *Crédit Mobilier* owning "**up to three newspapers at once**" and suing hostile editors — Aycard's verdict applied to both houses: a credit institution as "**a compound of monopolies to be cornered by high finance**" **[S: EBSCO; Ferguson]**. Press management was the industry's standard weapon, not a family signature; the family's distinction was doing it *better and longer*.

## PART 6 — THE GOVERNANCE CONSTITUTION

### 6.1 The 1810 compact (mechanics)

- **Ten-year, non-cancellable** term — locking in the sons **[S: Backhaus/Ferguson]**.
- Mayer's reserved powers: he alone could **withdraw capital, hire and fire, and had the final say**; profits allocated by capital share; joint management of the firm **[S]**.
- The injunction that made it a dynasty: the deathbed command preserved by the family — "**Amschel, keep your brothers together and you will become the richest people in Germany**" **[B: Ferguson]**. Every later compact, pacte, and SCA article is this sentence rewritten in legal code.
- The will's appendage: daughters and sons-in-law excluded from the firm, with the endogamy incentive that followed (Volume I, §7.5).

### 6.2 The 1815 federal compact (the machine's true constitution)

- Capital restructured collectively to **£500,000**; balances moved between houses through **£50,000 promissory notes** from Nathan to his brothers — the documented internal-wire mechanism.
- Shares: **Nathan 27%, Amschel 20, Salomon 20, Carl 16, James 16**; expenses charged against **London's** revenues; **net profits shared equally** — the ingenious mechanism that compensated the outlying houses for operating in smaller markets while keeping London's centrality **[S: Ferguson]**.
- The result, documented: house capital grew **£336,000 → £1,772,000 in three years** (the consol speculation); the weighting was later rebalanced further toward Nathan — to Carl's documented acknowledgment: "**We owe everything, really everything to him**" **[S]**.
- Corti's clauses: daughters/sons-in-law had **no right to see the books**; any partner who sued the firm had to **deposit a penalty first** — the anti-vexatious-litigation lock **[B: Corti]**.

### 6.3 The women's backdoor (documented)

- **Nathan's will bound his sons, as sub-partners, "to give weight to (their mother) Hannah's opinions"** — a formal clause smuggling a woman's judgment into the decision structure a generation before any statute would **[B: Livingstone]**.
- The women's documented system-functions: hosting as intelligence work (every dinner acquiring "items of news"), electoral choreography, code-breaking in the courier traffic, stock trading on their own accounts (Gutle's bills; Part 6 of this volume's companion volume).

### 6.4 The breach file: 1836 (governance under stress)

- Nathan committed the house to a **~£15-million-franc advance to Spain without his partners' consent** — the documented unauthorized-advance breach **[S: Ferguson]**.
- Salomon's damage control, preserved: a letter written for Metternich's eyes (via Wertheimstein) presenting Nathan's £2 million Spanish-bond dump as "**vengeance**," heaping abuse on his own brother ("**a child in politics… can hardly spell his own name**") to prove Viennese loyalty — while, in the same weeks, Salomon and James lobbied Talleyrand, Guizot, Broglie, and Louis-Philippe to contain the crisis ("**not a single farthing**" more to Spain) **[S: Ferguson, letters]**.
- The lesson the system drew: the equal-profit mechanism survived a London-Vienna breach — the constitution bent and held **[S]**.

### 6.5 The agency-control template: Belmont 1838 (verbatim principle)

The Archive preserves Belmont's position under constraint: the family would "**act for us in this situation and capacity**… "**if your activity of mind requires a wider sphere, you are at liberty to leave**" **[A: Archive]** — the documented doctrine: agents serve the network's scope, not their own; expansion beyond mandate is dischargeable. The template's modern echo and its failure mode are in Volume II (Bumi).

### 6.6 The succession mechanics, modern (documented)

- **SCA Articles 11.2–11.3:** general-partner decisions require **unanimity**; "exceptional transactions" — anything challenging the group's **independence, the family's attachment, the name, or the financial nature** of the group — require general-partner approval **regardless of shareholder votes**: constitutional entrenchment of the family's four non-negotiables **[A: SCA articles]**.
- The **commandite** structure: **Rothschild & Co Gestion** as general partner with **unlimited liability** — leadership legitimacy purchased with personal risk exposure ("the family's right to lead is grounded in its willingness to bear the highest level of personal risk" — the group's own framing) **[A/B]**.
- The **Edmond branch's** instruments: Benjamin's **65.94%** holding; **succession pactes**; the documented time-bomb of **Camillia** (~17% — Edmond's extramarital daughter's holding, per *Le Figaro*); the "everything returns to the four daughters" architecture — the four daughters born **1995–2002**, the generational pivot the Swiss press framed as the dynasty's "100% feminine" question; Ariane's minimum 25% by Swiss law; the family lawyer **Bottge** declining to discuss the pactes' terms — the documented opacity around the very document that will execute the next succession **[S/B: Le Figaro; 24heures]**.
- The **2023 take-private** as a governance choice: no quarterly reporting, faster succession, discretion (the delisting case-study record) — the modern expression of the 1810 compact's original logic: **the firm is a family instrument, not a public one** **[A/B]**.

## PART 7 — THE INFLUENCE VENUES: FIVE CHANNELS, ONE SYSTEM

### 7.1 The dinner (primary venue)

Heinrich Friedrich Karl vom und zum Stein's envoy **Smidt, 1820, documented:** "**no financial transaction of any importance could be carried through without the cooperation of these people, they had to be treated as friends**" — and the dinners acquired "**items of news**" as a matter of course **[S: Ferguson, citing Smidt]**. The mechanism: market access required social intimacy; social intimacy was manufactured at scale.

### 7.2 The country-house system (Waddesdon, documented operating model)

- Ferdinand's house (built 1874–83); **14–20 guests per weekend, May–September**; guest lists spanning **Edward VII, Gladstone, Queen Victoria (1890)**; a staff of **24**. The documented mechanics of a Waddesdon weekend: guests arrived Friday, shot or toured the gardens Saturday, talked Sunday — with the house's wine cellar, the celebrated kitchen, and the French furniture doing the soft work that no agenda could. The venue's documented political uses went well beyond hospitality: in **1913** it hosted the secret meeting between the Prince of Wales and Alice Keppel — the arrangement of a royal affair conducted on family ground — and across the decades its guest book functioned as a directory of everyone the system needed to know, recorded in the house's own visitor books **[B: Grokipedia-sourced from Waddesdon/NT records; graded B/C]**. The postscript is itself instructive: the **1957** bequest to the National Trust, with the Rothschild Foundation (Jacob as chair) managing it — the influence venue gracefully converted into heritage, and the family retaining the curatorial hand **[B]**.

### 7.3 The sporting network (documented)

- **Mayer (1818–1874):** racing colours registered **1843**; **Palace House bought at Queen Victoria's 1857 auction**; "**The Baron's Year**" (1871 — four of five classics); General Peel's Commons speech on the sixteen-stone horses; Jockey Club membership.
- **Leopold (1845–1917):** Jockey Club **elected 1891**; the "**Mr Acton**" pseudonym; **St Amant's 1904 Derby** (thunderstorm, "led him in with beaming face"); the **Russian government's purchase offer for St Amant — declined** — a *state* bidding for a family horse; **851 races won and £367,434 in winnings over 38 years**; the Eclipse prize fund he endowed (1886).
- **Evelyn:** Jockey Club **elected 1978**; chairman of United Racecourses 1977–94; the **Centaurs polo team's Queen's Cup**.
- The mechanism: racing and polo were where the aristocracy, the turf, and the money met without agenda papers — influence in its deniable form. (And the file's dark footnote: Louis was arrested in **1938 en route to a polo match**.)

### 7.4 The correction file: Tranby Croft (documented)

The 1890–91 baccarat scandal's Rothschild content is **proximity, not presence**: the Prince of Wales's Marlborough House set — Alfred de Rothschild was its intimate (Austro-Hungarian consul-general, CVO) — but the documented guest list contains **no Rothschild**. The claim of presence circulates; the record denies it. Registered as a closed correction **[S: Havers/Grayson/Whitehead; B]**.

### 7.5 The communal channel (documented)

The system's influence ran through the Jewish community's own institutions — the certification machine applied to communal life:

- **The Chief Rabbinate election, 1 December 1844:** three City synagogues elected **N. M. Adler (Hanover)** over Hirschfeld and Samson Raphael Hirsch by **121 of 143 votes**; the documented mechanics — London's synagogues held ~70% of the electing votes, and **Lionel controlled the bloc**, choosing the Hanover candidate (the family's old Oppenheimer/elector city; the Duke of Cambridge, Hanover's viceroy, the nexus). The payoff: the Rabbinate then sat with the family for **66 years** — Adler (1845–90), succeeded by his son **Hermann** (1890–1911), a Rothschild on his mother's side **[S/B: the election record; Times of Israel account]**.
- **The AIU presidency and the Alliance school network** (Edmond; Volume II and Part 12) — the same lock in Paris and the Mediterranean **[S]**.
- **The mechanism, stated:** communal office was elective; elections ran through institutions the family funded; funding purchased the institution; the institution conferred standing that translated outward into society and state. A second ledger, running beside the first.

### 7.6 The intelligence-world channel (Victor Rothschild, documented)

- **Cambridge Apostles**, elected **12 November 1932** (alongside Burgess, per Perry); the **5 Bentinck Street** flat lent to Burgess; Blunt's introduction to Teresa Mayor; the **£100 Poussin loan** (the painting later sold for £100,000).
- **MI5:** B1C head; bomb disposal; the **George Medal (1944)**; **Operation Fifth Column** (Eric Roberts embedded in the BUF); the **Pastorius** investigation; disinformation work.
- Post-war: ARC chairman; Royal Society; Shell research director; Thatcher's security adviser; N M Rothschild chairman.
- **The Fifth Man file:** questioned 1964, cleared; public denial letter (3 December 1986: "**I am not, and never have been, a Soviet agent**"); Perry's 1994 charges; the National Archives' interview file "**conspicuously missing**" — an anomaly preserved, not resolved; the consensus names Cairncross **[S/B]**.

Morgan–Communist nexus" and Gary Allen's "Milner financed the Bolsheviks on Rothschild's behalf" are **[X]** — documented as claims, separated from the S-grade record above. The documented JP Morgan apprenticeship is fact; the "controlled both sides" inference is not adopted.

## PART 8 — THE DIASPORA NETWORK: THE SYSTEM AS A WEB

### 8.1 The map (documented, EJ-grade)

Frankfurt-emigrant Jewish houses founded merchant banks across Europe and America: **Sterns (London, 1833), Salomons (1832), Montagu (1853), Speyers, Seligmans, Erlangers (1859), Japhet, Sassoon**; the **Bischoffsheim** web (Bamberger co-founding **Deutsche Bank 1870** and **Paribas 1872**); **Oppenheim** (Cologne) and the **Fould** and **Pereire** houses in Paris **[S: EJ "Banking"]**. The Rothschilds sat at the apex of this web — its largest node, its principal state creditor, its correspondent of first resort.

### 8.2 The apex fact

**From 1889 to 1901, every Russian loan issued in Paris ran through the Rothschild bank** — documented in the EJ's banking history **[S]**. A single family's name interposed between the Tsar and the French investing public for twelve years: the certification machine (Part 2) at its documented maximum. (The documented endpoint of that lock — the 1905–06 refusal and the Schiff-led counter-arrangement — remains **B-grade pending**; Anomaly #9.)

### 8.3 The clerk diaspora (the academy effect)

The houses trained the continent's bankers: **Hermann Wallich** left the Frankfurt house to become a founding director of **Deutsche Bank** (its Japan/China operations); **Ludwig Bamberger** — Bischoffsheim-trained, Rothschild-connected — co-founded the same bank and wrote its charter; **Carl Meyer** ran the London house's De Beers liaison (the 1888 letters confirming the Rhodes funding path) and sat on De Beers' board; **Abraham Wolff** anchored Bleichröder; **Abraham Oppenheim** (Cologne) bridged the Rhine web **[B: DB historiography; NHRM letters; flagged for monograph-grade confirmation]**. The mechanism: the family's *internal training* was the industry's external labor market — the system reproduced itself through its alumni.

### 8.4 The intermarriage web

The marriage map of Volume I (Wetzlar, Oppenheim, Kaz-Kalonymos, Lechnich, Schnapper, Gans) extended forward into the banking diaspora itself — the Oppenheim–Montefiore–Goldschmidt–Hanover links, the Behrends–Oppenheimer web — so that correspondent banking and cousinhood tracked each other: **credit followed kinship, and kinship followed credit** **[S/B]**.

### 8.5 How the web actually cleared paper (the operational layer)

Structure is not operation. The web functioned, day to day, through three documented mechanisms: (1) **correspondent credit lines** — each house accepted the others' paper and extended balances, so a bill drawn on Hamburg could be settled in Naples without metal moving at all; (2) **kinship-based trust premia** — a draft on a cousin-house cleared at par or near it, where a stranger's paper carried a discount, which is precisely why the intermarriage web of §8.4 was worth building and maintaining; and (3) **the apex guarantee** — when a distant house's client strained the local web, the family's name stood behind it, converting a provincial firm's credit into something a London or Paris counterparty would accept. The system's documented pathology was the mirror image: when the apex failed (1931), the web's trust premia inverted overnight, and kinship could not clear what credit had **[S/B: the EJ banking survey; the 1931 record of Volume II]**.

## PART 9 — THE CHALLENGER WAR: CRÉDIT MOBILIER VS. THE SYSTEM (DOCUMENTED)

### 9.1 The challenger

The **Pereire brothers** founded the **Crédit Mobilier on 18 November 1852 — explicitly against the Rothschild–Talabot railway alliance**; James refused to subscribe. The model: a joint-stock bank lending long to industry against its own shares, permitted **10× leverage** by decree; warrants traded **995 → 2,110 francs** before listing; dividends reached **40% (1855)**; the empire spanned railways, shipping, insurance, gas — and **newspapers (up to three at once)** **[S/A: EBSCO; the decree record]**.

### 9.2 The counter-system

- The family's documented reply to CM's Austrian expansion was institutional, not rhetorical: **the founding of the Creditanstalt (1855)** as the blocking instrument in Vienna **[A/S: Greenfield, KCL]**.
- The war ran through the Bourse, the press, and the ministry for fifteen years; the 1856 government block on CM's 240-million note issue, the 1857 panic, and the 1866 crisis bled it; the **Banque de France forced the Pereires out in September 1867**; losses passed **47 million francs**. Both sides weaponized print — CM held "**up to three newspapers at once**" and sued hostile editors (the *Finance* editor Crampon), while Marx devoted his 1856 articles to the monster; Aycard's verdict applied to both houses alike **[S]**.
- The war's human texture on the system side: Stadion's Austrian program was "**ruthless**," and the family's loans financed the very occupation burden they then lobbied to relieve (Part 2.2) — the valve again **[S]**.
- The aftermath, documented and instructive: **the victors absorbed the loser's model** — deposit-backed universal banking through the **Crédit Lyonnais (1863)** and **Société Générale (1864)** — and the "cosmetic reconciliation between the emperor and the Rothschilds" (1859–60) showed the system's preference for absorbing challengers over destroying them **[S: EBSCO]**.

### 9.3 The war-profit coda

The **1870–71 war** against which CM had bet: "**did the Rothschild business no real harm and, because the bank managed the payment of the French indemnity, in the long run resulted in a profit**" **[S: EBSCO]**. The system profited from the catastrophe that killed its challenger — and collected the indemnity besides.

## PART 10 — THE STRESS-TEST RECORD: HOW THE SYSTEM BENT (AND WHERE IT BROKE)

| Year | Shock | Documented response | System lesson |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1825 | Latin-American bubble burst; two houses held the book | Family capital pledged while investors ran; the BoE–Banque de France gold lifeline routed through the house (£400k) **[A]** | Whole-book risk = systemic exposure; the certification brand compelled the rescue |
| 1836–37 | Spanish crisis; Nathan's unauthorized advance (~£15M francs) | Salomon's two-track damage control (Part 6.4) **[S]** | Equal-profit constitution bends, holds |
| 1848 | Revolutions; railway losses; war scares | The houses absorbed losses; the system's diversification (five capitals) carried it **[S/B]** | Geographical spread = survival |
| 1859 | Franco-Austrian war; panics (Austrian 5%s at 49) | James's Plombières-informed positioning (Part 4.3) **[S/B]** | Political intelligence pays only if the desk acts |
| 1866 | Vienna crash; CM bleeding | James's Vienna buying; the CA weapon consolidated **[B/S]** | Contrarian liquidity at the moment of maximum fear |
| 1870–71 | War on the system's home ground | Managed the indemnity; long-run profit **[S]** | The system's preferred catastrophe is one that ends with a transfer it administers |
| 1882 | Union Générale crash (the Catholic counter-bank) | The family emerged stronger; Zola's *L'Argent* is the novel of the episode **[S]** | Counter-systems fail on their own leverage |
| 1890 | Baring crisis | Natty cabled Paris; the **Banque de France swapped £3M in gold for Treasury bills**; Russia added £1.5M; the rescue armed; **Alfred de Rothschild sat inside the Bank of England (director 1868–1889)** through it all **[S/A]** | The family as the transmission line *into* the central bank — twice (1825, 1890) |
| 1907 | US panic | **No Rothschild role documented** — Morgan, the Clearinghouse, the Treasury, the Bank of Montreal, the Bank of France **[S]** | The system's absence is as diagnostic as its presence |
| 1914–18 | The Great War | No American branch; the transatlantic wire broken; London's dominance eclipsed **[S: Ferguson]** | The system's geography failed before its finances did |
| 1931 | Creditanstalt | **The detonator** (Volume II, Part 6.1): hidden losses 7×; the bailout arithmetic; Europe-wide chain **[S]** | Concentration in one jurisdiction's flagship = the terminal weakness; the wire ran the other way |

The pattern the table shows: **the system survived information shocks, governance breaches, panics, and wars; it broke twice — on geography (1914) and on jurisdiction-concentration (1931).** Its dangers were never its enemies; they were its addresses.

## PART 11 — THE SYSTEM'S EFFECTS ON OTHERS (DOCUMENTED EXTERNALITIES)

1. **The certification premium, collected from public borrowers:** debut discounts and commissions charged to *states* for the service of being made creditworthy — the 1818 Prussians paid it; so did every successor sovereign **[S]**.
2. **Occupation financing:** the Neapolitan loans funded the Austrian occupation the family then lobbied to end (Part 2.2) — the system's output included the financing of burdens, with the family's margin at both ends **[S]**.
3. **Market-information asymmetry:** inner-circle allotments and convergence plays meant the certification machine transferred value from the outside investor to the connected one — the documented "guaranteed success" of a house issue is another name for a structural edge for insiders **[S]**.
4. **Monopoly rents:** mercury (86 years), diamonds (the syndicate), copper, uranium proximity, railways with state guarantees — each documented in Volume II Part 3; the rent mechanism was consistent: commodity or infrastructure + state concession + family certification **[S/A]**.
5. **Crisis transmission:** when the flagship failed in 1931, the system's failure propagated through the entire European banking structure — the family was not merely a victim of the depression; its institution was a documented vector **[S]**.
6. **The advisory era's structural conflicts (modern):** sovereign-advisory mandates while the wider network holds positions in the same paper — the industry's own framework concedes the deficiency (Volume II, Part 2.5) **[A]**.
7. **The counterweight cases, recorded for balance:** the Texas slave-bond refusal (1830s); the Russian-loan refusal (1905, B-grade pending); the 1860–62 US crisis gold (B-grade, flagged); the documented refusal to finance certain wars; and the system's consistent preference for *orderly* markets — the family's interest in stability is structural, not philanthropic, and the record shows both halves **[S/B; flagged where pending]**.

## PART 12 — PHILANTHROPY AS SYSTEM: THREE GENERATIONS OF THE SAME MACHINE

1. **The communal lock (19th century):** schools and synagogues as leadership instruments — the Evelina de Rothschild School (Jerusalem, 1864; £800/year; "the best Jewish Girls School in Palestine" by WWI); the Frankfurt foundations of the will's 5,000-gulden tithe; the London communal offices. The mechanism: *fund the institution, hold the institution* — the same certification logic applied to communal life **[A/B]**.
2. **Edmond's Palestine model (1882–1925+):** philanthropy run as a development corporation with the family as holding company. The step-by-step, as documented: (1) **survey** — agronomists, doctors, and engineers sent ahead to assess land, water, and settlement viability, on the PICA payroll; (2) **acquisition** — land purchased outright (the family's holdings in Palestine ultimately reached an estimated 2–3% of the territory, per Volume II); (3) **development** — wineries, farms, and villages built with the family's capital *and* its administration — the **pekidim** (administrators) ran the colonies as a business, not a charity; (4) **the transfer instruments** — the investments passed to the **ICA (1899)** and were refounded as **PICA (1924)**, formal vehicles letting the family step back from daily management without surrendering direction; (5) **the parallel French portfolio** — the same method applied to science (Institut Henri Poincaré, the pre-CNRS institutes, Casa Velázquez) and to education ($300,000 for the Tel Aviv school building under the AIU). The principle unifying all five steps: *never give a gift where you can endow an institution; never endow an institution without a seat on its board* — the certification machine (Part 2) running in the nonprofit register **[S/B]**.
3. **The modern human-capital strategy (stated on the foundation's own site):** the Edmond de Rothschild Foundation (Israel) reports partnerships with **37 academic institutions and six government ministries**, 1,000 scholarships, 40,000 participants — and states its goal as building **cadres of leaders** and alumni networks reducing periphery-center disparities. The mechanism, said plainly by the institution itself: **leadership pipelines** — influence through human capital, with the network as the asset **[A: foundation publications]**.
4. **Yad Hanadiv / Ramat HaNadiv:** the family's Israeli foundation funding national infrastructure (the Knesset, the Supreme Court, science facilities — Volume II) with formalized funding cycles — matching grants, calls, evaluation — the certification machine's nonprofit descendant **[A/B]**.

The three generations share one architecture: *identify the institution that confers legitimacy, fund it durably, and hold the relationship.*

## PART 13 — THE MODERN ENGINE: THE FEE MACHINE (DOCUMENTED ARITHMETIC)

### 13.1 The composition (group consolidated accounts)

- **2024:** M&A advisory **€951.2M**; financing advisory **€747.7M**; wealth & asset-management portfolio fees **€696.9M**; Five Arrows **€228.7M**; banking fees €7.6M; other €11.9M → **total fee income €2,644.1M** **[A]**.
- **2025:** M&A **€1,049.1M**; financing **€700.5M**; WAM **€801.9M**; Five Arrows **€250.1M** → **€2,822.0M**; carried interest €48.6M **[A]**.
- Composition: **~37% M&A, ~28% wealth, ~25% financing, ~9% funds** — a pure fee annuity; the group deliberately holds no principal balance sheet against clients (the "independent advice" positioning, Volume II) **[A/S]**. What the annuity means structurally, stated plainly: the 1810 machine sold certification against its own capital, and died of the concentration risk that entailed (Part 14); the modern machine sells the same certification but rents the capital risk to clients, funds, and partners — the fee is paid *because* the advice carries no balance-sheet conflict, and the absence of a balance sheet is precisely what keeps the fee coming. The year-over-year movement documents the model's health: fee income up **€178 million (6.7%)** from 2024 to 2025, with wealth management (+15%) and M&A (+10%) both growing — the certification brand, two centuries on, still clearing at a premium **[A]**. The industry-standard fee ladder against which the bank prices (success-fee percentages declining with deal size — roughly 4–12% on the smallest mandates through 2–5% mid-market to 1–2% at the largest, the classic Lehman-formula structure) is the mechanic beneath the €1 billion M&A line **[C — industry-standard structure; the bank's own terms are private]**.

### 13.2 The subsidiaries' texture

- **Rothschild & Co Bank AG (Zurich), 2024:** income **CHF 238.6M**, net profit **CHF 37.4M**, commission income +13.6% to CHF 134.5M, staff 460 — the small, discreet, profitable Swiss footprint descending from the old Bank AG line **[A]**.
- **RIT Capital** (~£4.7bn; family ~20.9% across four vehicles) and the **Five Arrows** complex (>€34bn AUM) run the family's own money alongside client money — the two-book system, private and public, as it has existed since 1810 **[A]**.

### 13.3 What survived and what changed

Survived: the partnership constitution, the name-as-credit, the sovereign-advisory desk, the fee-for-certification core. Changed: the information moat (dead 1835→), the commodity monopolies (fragmented), the balance-sheet risk (outsourced to clients and funds), and the geography (Paris-led, three houses). The system is the same machine running different software.

## PART 14 — FAILURE MECHANICS: THE SYSTEM'S OWN WEAK POINTS

1. **Transmission-speed dependency:** the moat expired on schedule (1851) — any advantage built on being faster dies by wire **[S]**.
2. **Concentration risk:** Naples at 15% of one house (1826); the CA at the center of one country's banking (1931) — the certification machine's insurance function forced the balance sheet into the very risks it certified **[S]**.
3. **Governance breaches:** the 1836 unauthorized advance; the 1980 Jacob–Evelyn schism; the Bumi minority-structure failure; the 2015–18 compliance cascade — the system's human parts periodically override its constitutional parts, and the correction is always expensive **[S/A]**.
4. **Agency drift:** Belmont's 1838 ultimatum defined the control; Bumi defined the failure mode when the control was absent **[A/S]**.
5. **Jurisdiction risk:** 1938, 1982, 2015 (the DOJ/FINMA cascade) — the system's terminal exposures are political, not financial **[A]**.
6. **Secrecy vs. disclosure:** the 1810 compact's secrecy clauses were an asset under courier-era conditions and became the system's single largest liability under disclosure-era conditions — the enforcement record of Volume II is, mechanistically, the old secrecy architecture meeting the new transparency regime **[A/S]**.
6a. **The cipher was not unbreakable:** the Austrian authorities **deciphered the family's Judendeutsch correspondence** in the early nineteenth century — the secrecy architecture's own operators read by the state's code-breakers, decades before the disclosure regimes of Volume II made secrecy itself the liability **[S/B: encyclopedia.com/Ferguson record]**.

---

## PART 0 — HOW TO READ THIS MAP

The trilogy documents connections in prose; this volume renders them as a **graph**. The rules of the other volumes carry over, with graph conventions added:

- **Nodes** are people, families, firms, states, or institutions. Each is registered in Part 1 with its class and pedigree.
- **Edges** are documented relationships, typed by mechanism (Part 2). An edge exists in this map **only where the trilogy documents it** — with the pedigree grade of its evidence at the point of listing.
- **Myth-corrupted nodes** (Part 1, class M) are mapped *as claims*: the node exists in the public record, but the documented connection is an absence, a correction, or a refutation. Mapping the false edges is part of mapping the network, because the myth system itself is a network — one with documented origins (Dairnvaell 1846; Drumont 1886; Mullins 1952) and documented carriers.
- **Eras** are given so the map can be read longitudinally: the network of 1815 is not the network of 1914, and neither is the network of 2026.
- **Degree notes:** where an edge's evidence is contested or thin, the edge carries a flag rather than a silent inclusion.

---

## PART 0A — THE FIVE-MINUTE VERSION (PLAIN LANGUAGE)

Before the tables, the story. A **network** is just this: people and institutions as **points**, and their documented relationships as **lines**. If you drew your own family this way — you, your parents, your spouse's family, your employer, your bank — every line would be a real, checkable fact. This map does that for the Rothschild world, with one strict rule: *a line exists only where the documents exist.* No line is drawn because someone famous said so.

**One example, walked slowly.** In 1770, Mayer Amschel Rothschild married Gutle Schnapper. That's one line — a marriage line. Gutle's father, Wolf Salomon Schnapper, was a small Frankfurt banker who happened to do business with a man named Buderus, who managed the fortune of the Landgrave of Hesse — then the richest prince in Germany. In 1790, Schnapper introduced Buderus to his son-in-law. That's a second line — an introduction. Within a decade, Mayer Amschel was handling the prince's money across Europe. That is what this map calls an **edge**: not a rumor of influence, but a documented connection with a date and a source, through which something — money, information, access — demonstrably flowed.

Now scale that up. This volume draws well over a hundred such edges, connecting the best part of two hundred points: the five Rothschild houses, thirteen intermarried families, a dozen allied banking families, twenty-seven states, the agents and court officials in between, the corporations they financed, the newspapers they leaned on, the regulators who later investigated them — and, separately and clearly marked, the *false* connections (the Illuminati, the Federal Reserve "ownership," the space laser), mapped as claims with their real history attached, because you cannot understand the family's story without understanding the legend that shadowed it.

**Three plain promises:** every line carries its evidence grade ([A] strongest, [X] hostile myth); every technical word is defined where you first meet it, and again in the word-list at Part 2A; and wherever the evidence is thin or missing, the line says so instead of pretending.

## PART 2A — THE WORDS THIS MAP USES (PLAIN DEFINITIONS)

- **Node** — one point on the map: a person, family, firm, state, or institution.
- **Edge** — one documented line between two points, with a date and a source.
- **Pedigree tag** — the reliability grade printed right after each claim. **[A]** = archives, court records, official documents. **[S]** = scholarly work. **[B]** = serious secondary sources. **[C]** = lead-grade, single-source. **[X]** = hostile myth literature — quoted only to show what the myth is and where it fails.
- **Correspondent banking** — banks doing business through each other: one bank accepts another's paper, moves its money, vouches for its client. Before telephones, this *was* international banking — a web of favors and accepted IOUs.
- **Underwriting** — guaranteeing that a government's loan will be placed with investors; the guarantor takes the risk and charges the fee.
- **Certification** — the family's core business in one word: their name on a loan made the loan trustworthy, and they charged states for that trust (Volume III explains the machinery).
- **Apex creditor** — not the owner of the network, but the point everyone eventually routes through: the bank that can always pay.
- **Syndicate** — a group of banks sharing one big loan.
- **Agency / proxy** — a local representative bound to the house: the family's man in a city where it has no office (Belmont in New York, Bleichröder in Berlin).
- **Enforcement** — regulators, courts, and prosecutors: the counter-network that only appears on the map from the 1930s onward, and thickens sharply after 2015.
- **Betweenness** (used once, in Part 4) — how often a point lies *on the path* between other points. High betweenness means "the person in the middle" — everyone needs them to reach everyone else.

---

## PART 3 — THE EDGE LIST (THE COMPLETE DOCUMENTED MAP)

**How to read one line, in plain terms.** Take this entry:

> *R → Prussia (1818 £5M template; 1822 £3.5M) | U | 1818–22 | S/A | III·3*

It says: a Rothschild house (that's the "R") made a documented connection to the Kingdom of Prussia — specifically by underwriting its loans, five million pounds in 1818, which became the template for the business, and £3.5 million more in 1822. The **U** is the edge type (underwriting — see the table in Part 2). The date is 1818–22. The grade **S/A** means the evidence is scholarly and archive-grade. And **III·3** means the full story, with quotes and sources, lives in Volume III, Part 3 of this file series. One line here; a chapter there.

Format throughout: **Source → Target | Type | Era | Pedigree | Volume·Part**. The house node "R" stands for the relevant Rothschild house(s) of the era.

### 3.1 Kin and training (the base layer)
- R ← Hahn (ancestral branch) | M | pre-1530–1634 | A/B | I·3
- R ← Kaz/Kalonymos (Schönche, m. Naftali Hirz 1634) | M | 1634 | A | I·5

- R ← Buxbaum (Schönche, m. Moses Kalman) | M | pre-1733 | A | I·5
- R ← Lechnich (Schönche, m. Amschel Moses; Sarchen, m. Moses Amschel) | M | 18th c. | A | I·5
- R ← Schnapper (Gutle, m. Mayer Amschel 1770) | M | 1770 | A | I·6

Read as a graph, the myth network has the same anatomy as the real one: originators, brokers, and amplifiers — and one documented property the real network lacks: *immortality without accuracy.* Every carrier above is dead or discredited; every claim survives.

## PART 4 — WHAT THE MAP SHOWS, IN PLAIN WORDS

Six findings, stated the way you'd tell a friend.

**1. The family sits at the top without owning the machine.** The thickest cluster on the map is not the Rothschilds — it is the loose web of allied Jewish banking families (the diaspora class) that moved money around the family whenever they could. The Rothschilds were the point everyone eventually passed through, not the owner of the road. This is exactly why the conspiracy claims fail on the evidence: you cannot "own the Federal Reserve" or "found the BIS" in a network that is mostly other people's relationships — and the documents show the family's real position was stronger than ownership anyway: everyone *needed* them, which is better than controlling them and far harder to prove in a pamphlet.

**2. The most powerful points are the people in the middle.** When map-readers measure "betweenness" — how often a point lies on the only path between two others — the winners are not the family and not the kings. They are the in-betweeners: Buderus, the Kassel official who introduced Mayer Amschel to the Landgrave's fortune; Bleichröder, Bismarck's banker in Berlin; Belmont, the family's man in New York; Gentz, the journalist who managed newspapers; and, in a darker register, the lawyer Kathy Ruemmler, introduced to the family by Jeffrey Epstein in 2014. Every generation of this system has its middlemen. The map shows the same job filled for two hundred years.

**3. They sold trust far more often than they bought control.** Count the lines: fifty sovereign loans underwritten in London between 1815 and 1859, against only a handful of seats on corporate boards. The family mostly did not *buy* companies — it *made governments lendable* and charged for the service. The modern bank, earning 37% of its fees from deal advice and 28% from managing rich clients' money, is the same shape in modern dress: a firm that sells judgment, not capital.

**4. The myth network is a real network — that's why it won't die.** The false connections (the Illuminati link, the Fed "ownership," the Waterloo legend) are not scattered rumors. Mapped, they form their own web with documented carriers — a pamphlet in 1846, a campaign book in 1886, a conspiracy text in 1956 — passing the same stories down like family silver. False lines imitate true ones, cluster like true ones, and transmit like true ones. That is why each one in this map carries its own verdict and history attached: the only known antidote is keeping the dispositions next to the claims.

**5. The regulators only arrive late — then all at once.** Before 1914 the map shows almost no lines to courts and regulators. Between 1931 and 1982 come the catastrophes (the Creditanstalt collapse, the Nazi seizure, the French nationalization). After 2015 the cluster turns dense: the US Justice Department, the Swiss and Luxembourg regulators, French investigators raiding a Paris office in 2026, a congressional committee hearing testimony in July of that year. The documented story of this family is, in map form, the story of one network meeting its counter-network.

**6. The marriages are the foundation.** Remove the kinship lines and whole layers of the map fall with them: the correspondent web, the agency layer, half the political access. The family married into the families it banked with, and banked with the families it married into — credit followed kinship, and kinship followed credit. Everything else on this map — the loans, the concessions, the houses, the scandals — stands on that bedrock.

---

## PART 5 — THE MAP AS DATA (FOR MACHINES AS WELL AS READERS)

Everything in Part 3 is written in one consistent shorthand so that a computer can read it too: paste the list into any network-drawing program (the free ones are called Gephi, NetworkX, and Cytoscape) and the map draws itself. Four conventions cover the whole list: **R** always means "whichever Rothschild house was operating at that time"; an **arrow** means the flow has a documented direction (money, introductions, influence moving from one point to the other); an arrow marked **→|** marks a line that *ended* in a documented event — a liquidation, a nationalization, a conviction; and any line tagged **F** is a claim rather than a fact, kept in the data so the false map can be studied beside the true one. The clickable picture of this map accompanies the volume — filters for each connection type, and a click on any point opens its record.

---

## PART 6 — OPEN EDGES (LINES WE COULD NOT YET DRAW, AND WHY)

*Seven places where the evidence runs out before the line can. Each says what would settle it and where that answer lives.*

1. **Reuters** — the rumor that the family controlled the news agency could never be verified (it failed three separate checks); a single documented instruction beyond ordinary client subscription would reopen the line (Reuters company archives).
2. **The Schiff counter-arrangement (1905–06)** — moderately documented but unconfirmed here; proof would redraw one of the map's strongest lines, the family's twelve-year lock on Russian loans (1905–06), as a Schiff–Russia line instead (Schiff family papers).
3. **The uranium cartel's inner committee** — the cartel itself is proven to the House of Lords; what is missing is any document putting a family member inside its decision room. The minutes would either draw that line or rule it out (US National Archives).
4. **The supposed 1899 bankers' convention** — the story exists in exactly one conspiracy book; one primary document — an invitation, a minutes page, a hotel register — would turn the myth line into a real one. Until then it stays in the myth class, with that book named as its only carrier.
5. **Gentz's retainer** — we know the family leaned on the editor of a major German newspaper *through* the statesman Gentz; we do not know whether Gentz was paid for it. His account books would upgrade the line from "leaned on" to "bought" (Vienna archives).
6. **The Cook Islands 2016 shift** — a press claim that assets moved after a new disclosure law could not be verified in three attempts; confirmation would add a fresh line to the family's documented offshore cluster (Portcullis/TrustNet records).
7. **The Poyais intermediaries** — the 1820s fraud in which bonds for a fictional country were sold on the London market; who exactly enabled it, beyond the ministers who looked away, would fill out the map's one documented case of certification without verification.

## PART 8A — THE MAP IN FIVE SNAPSHOTS (ERA SLICES)

*The same network at five moments — watch nodes appear, edges thicken, and clusters die.*

- **1815 — ~30 nodes.** One family, four brothers, the courier chain, the elector, three allied families, two states that matter. The star is forming.
- **1850 — ~80 nodes.** The certification star at full brightness: five houses, a dozen kin lines, Bleichröder and Belmont embedded, the press edges live, the first corporate concessions. The information moat has two years to live.
- **1900 — ~120 nodes.** The apex: the Russia lock, the BoE seat, De Beers and Rio Tinto, the diaspora web at maximum reach. The map has never been denser — and the two missing edges (no American branch, no syndicated risk) are already written into its future.
- **1931–1938 — subtraction.** The Creditanstalt node fires; the Vienna cluster is seized at gunpoint; the map loses its continental half in seven years. What survives is the name and the kin-web.
- **2026 — two clusters and a counter-network.** Three houses (Paris-led, Geneva, London trust), the fee engine, the philanthropy nodes, the media stake closed in March — and across the map, the densest cluster in the family's documented history: regulators, prosecutors, courts, and committees, mapped edge by edge in Part 3.10.

## PART 8 — TIMELINE OF THE MAP'S SHAPE (SELECTED)

*One breath per era: watch the map grow from a single ghetto street into a web across Europe, fragment, re-form — and finally meet the regulators. Read each dash as one era's shape.*

1530–1634 — the kin base (Hahn; Kaz; the Hinterpfann). 1608–1785 — kin-web thickening (Oppenheim, Wetzlar, Schnapper, Gans). 1757–90 — the agent layer forms (Oppenheim, Buderus). 1798–1815 — the five-house star appears. 1815–59 — the certification star dominates (50 loans). 1836–1930s — the agency century (Belmont). 1850s–90s — the proxy apex (Bleichröder; the Russia lock). 1868–89 — a family seat inside the BoE. 1880s–1914 — the corporate cluster (De Beers; Rio Tinto; rails; Baku). 1914–45 — the star fragments (no US branch; 1931; 1938). 1945–82 — the reconstitution (Israel; Economist; the Jockey Club thread). 1982–2015 — the modern nodes assemble. 2015–26 — the counter-network's dense cluster (DOJ–FINMA–CSSF–Luxembourg–Paris raid–Oversight testimony) — and the Economist edge closes.

## PART 0 — HOW TO READ THIS VOLUME

Same protocol, current tense. The pedigree key is unchanged: **[A]** archives, court records, official documents; **[S]** scholarly and quality investigative press; **[B]** serious secondary; **[C]** lead-grade; **[L]** lore; **[X]** hostile myth — documented as claims, never adopted. Three rules carry over: **no adopted theories** (where interpretation outruns documents, the document and the interpretation are printed separately); **anomalies are registered** (Part 12); **nothing is omitted** — including the cases that died quietly, the names that never made the papers, and the null results.

Scope and method. This volume covers the living file from roughly January 2024 through 20 September 2026, assembled in a dedicated series of deep dives ending in a verification run. Where a fact rests on a document identifier (an **EFTA Bates number** — EFTA is the *Epstein Files Transparency Act*, the 2025 US law that forced the Justice Department's release, and a Bates number is the tracking stamp on each released page; the same applies to court dockets and charity registers), the identifier is printed — because in this file, the document *is* the evidence, and anyone reading should be able to walk the same path. Where a fetch failed and a fact rests on a quoted excerpt, the grade says so.

One framing sentence before the machinery: this is not an account of a family in decline or a machine triumphant. It is an account of a network under simultaneous expansion (record fees, record AUM — assets under management, the client money these firms invest on their clients' behalf — and a historic IPO position) and unprecedented documentary exposure (a thousand-plus released documents, four open investigations on three continents, and a legislature suing the Justice Department for more). Both halves are documented. Neither is interpreted.

## PART 4 — CONFLICTS OF INTEREST (DOCUMENTED)

1. **The sovereign desk's declared line (S):** on Ukraine — *"At that point, we made the decision to advise the public sector in Ukraine, and not Russia. Being mindful of possible conflicts, we made that decision at the time and we stuck to it since then"* (Charbit, IFR). Adviser since April 2017; the desk's book includes Chad, Benin, Germany. The structural conflict the industry concedes — advising distressed sovereigns while the wider network holds their paper — applies to the model, and the bank's own published policy treats "over-reliance on disclosure" as a deficiency (April 2023).
2. **The Epstein-advice conflict, dated (S/A):** a bank under a September 2015 contract to pay a convicted offender's company up to $25 million was simultaneously — per the same corpus — receiving his counsel on DOJ strategy, his introductions to European political figures, and his intercession in an employee severance. Each element is documented; the composite is the conflict.
3. **The hiring-after-plea (S):** Jonathan Kaye joined the bank the same month his six misdemeanors were pledged for dismissal (Part 6.7).
4. **The funding-plus-seat structure (S/B):** Eranda's grants to CFR/IISS/Chatham House against family seats on their boards (Part 3.1).
5. **The Exbury knot (S):** Marcus Agius — former Barclays chairman — chairs Exbury Gardens Limited while married to Kate de Rothschild; a marriage-and-governance overlap, documented, unadjudicated.
6. **The SpaceX exposure (A):** a 2.5%-of-NAV position in a *controlled company* (one whose founder holds majority voting power — 82.4% here — letting it claim exemptions from the usual board-independence rules) that the bank's trust carries below market — concentration risk by design.
7. **The 2015 strategy weekend (A/NA):** the bank's CEO, CFO, and counsel-adjacent advisers meeting a convicted offender to plan "DOJ, review legal vetting — bermuda… look closely at Luxembourg" — while the bank held client money from a 1MDB-tainted network. Documents verified; the meetings' product remains undocumented.

| US | Swiss Bank Program (DOJ); the NPA (non-prosecution agreement) mechanism — penalties and cooperation exchanged for the government declining to prosecute | EdR (Suisse) $45M; Rothschild Bank AG $11.5M (18 Dec 2015) | Non-prosecution agreements — prosecutions declined by design |

| US (SDNY) | Bankruptcy Code clawbacks (*In re Fairfield Sentry* — Madoff feeder) | Redeemer Litigation vs R&Co (11 June 2024 jurisdiction opinion — EGA "instructed BNP SS to invest in Madoff feeder funds with the express aim" of reaching BLMIS, knowing the 95% mandate) and EdR Suisse (15 July 2025 dismissal denied) | **Live** |
| UK | Consumer Credit Act 1974 s.140A (the provision letting courts reopen credit agreements that created an "unfair relationship" between lender and borrower) | *Carney v N M Rothschild* ([2018] EWHC 958) | Claim dismissed; leading case for "basis clauses" (contract terms in which the client formally declares the bank gave no advice) |
| Massachusetts | Limitations law (suits brought too late); the puffery doctrine | *Von Schönau-Riedweg* (95 Mass. App. Ct. 471; *remand* = sent back to the lower court for reconsideration, rulings 2021) | Disposed on limitations/puffery; von Plotho default $14.9M; post-2021 endgame open |

**How to read the column of outcomes.** They fall into four documented kinds: the *conviction* (Luxembourg — the bank, by agreement); the *charges* (Norway; the French individuals pending); the *declined-by-design* (the US NPAs — prosecutions traded for penalties, a lawful mechanism with a documented broker); and the *disposed* (dismissals on merits, limitations, or constitutional ruling — Parts 6.4–6.8). One row is not an outcome but a *live clawback* (SDNY). Note also what the table does not contain: no member of the banking family appears in it as a defendant, in any jurisdiction, in the current file. The defendants are banks, executives, employees, a name-alike inventor, and — in France — a diplomat. That distribution is itself a documented fact, stated without interpretation.

## PART 6 — THE DROPPED, THE FORGOTTEN, THE RESURRECTED (THE FULL CASE LEDGER)

*Every case in the file that died quietly, was traded away, or came back — who, what, where, when, why, how.*

### 6.1 The French Epstein investigation — forgotten 2022, reborn 2026, widening September (S)

*Who:* the Paris probe (opened August 2019); its central figure, Jean-Luc Brunel. *What happened:* Brunel found dead in his cell (February 2022); the case was dropped in 2023 with no other person charged; the parallel Gérald Marie probe closed the same year as time-barred. *Why it mattered:* the investigation died with its suspect — the classic forgotten case. *How it returned:* the DOJ's three-million-page release (late 2025); 14 February 2026 — a special magistrates' team with PNF integration; 18 February — prosecutor **Laurence Beccuau** opens two new preliminary investigations (sexual; financial) and reopens the old files, calling victims forward; 17 May — ~20 victims, ~10 entirely new; **11 September 2026 — the investigation widened, 13 new potential victims identified** (NYT). *Live and growing.*

### 6.2 The Aidan file — dropped twice, resurrected once (A/S)

The arc, dated: French Embassy in Israel 1998–2000; UN secondment July 2006–April 2013; Araud sent him back within 48 hours of the FBI dossier surfacing, expecting discipline that never came — dropped in 2013 (UN process suspended on resignation; no French action; the Canada flight); the 2020 Paris-brigade file (a *classement sans suite* — shelved without continuation — the same year; reasons still unverified, now assigned to the reactivated magistrates); the bank employment from early 2014; the Tobiano email of 13 April 2016; the dismissal of 12 September 2016; the Engie coda (2023–February 2026); the Neuilly search (26 February); resurrected February 2026 (Barrot's Article-40 *referral* — the French mechanism by which an official who learns of a possible crime must send it directly to prosecutors — within hours of publication; dual probes; the 20 March raid in Ariane's presence; the Quai IG report transmitted to justice, ~30 hearings). *Why it kept dying:* diplomatic-immunity complexity (2013); 2020 reasons sealed. *The silence:* no public development after 24 March 2026 — three consecutive checks, zero results; the silence is logged as a datum.

### 6.3 The NPA mechanism — non-prosecution bought, lawfully (A)

The DOJ Swiss Bank Program: EdR (Suisse) $45M and Rothschild Bank AG $11.5M (both 18 December 2015) bought non-prosecution — the legal mechanism by which the prosecutions were dropped. Documented in Volume II; here flagged as the structural dropped-case: the settlement Epstein then monetized within three days.

### 6.4 Carney v N M Rothschild — dismissed, and became the banks' shield (A)

[2018] EWHC 958 (Comm), Waksman QC, 1 May 2018 — the first reported case on CCA 1974 s.140A and "basis clauses": the expatriates' inheritance-tax scheme failed; the "basis clauses" (no-advice, no-reliance) defined the transaction and negated the advisory duty; the s.140A claim failed. Not dropped — dismissed on the merits; now the leading authority *for* banks' clause defenses. The clients bore the loss.

### 6.5 Von Schönau-Riedweg — death by limitations and puffery (A)

$39M into CET/CCI; the Appeals Court (2019) *vacated* (cancelled) the bank's jurisdictional dismissal (agency showing); Judge Brian A. Davis (2021) kept the bank in (rejecting *forum non conveniens* — the doctrine that the case belonged in a more convenient foreign court — and comity) — but the CET claims died on the statute of limitations (inquiry notice November 2008), the CCI claims as non-actionable puffery (the doctrine that promises of handsome profit are legally matters of opinion, not binding promises), Porter on summary judgment. Von Plotho's default: $14,946,979. *The open end:* the post-2021 disposition against the bank — settlement or trial, unreported.

### 6.6 Fortinvest v. Oblonsky — the decade stall (S/B)

Three jurisdictions, one stalled claim: the NY case tossed November 2021 (forum — contracts mandate Luxembourg); the Geneva criminal action dismissed on procedural grounds; the Luxembourg civil action (filed October 2017) still without a substantive ruling, *stayed* (paused by the judge) behind the Thewes criminal file. Park Slope, Brooklyn Pride, 8 June 2024: the punch (Micah Phillip, nose broken in four places), the shove; six misdemeanors; the self-defense claim (disputed). The plea (28 February 2025): guilty to all six, convertible to violations on 25 days' community service, three anger-management sessions, up to $50,000 restitution, expungement path — Judge Dale Fong-Frederick. The hiring the same month. The victim's sentence in the file: he should not "be in a position of power until he's able to be a better member of the community."

Conseil constitutionnel, décision 81-132 DC (16 January 1982): the compensation mechanism *censured* (struck down as unconstitutional) for violating the "juste et préalable" (just and prior) indemnity; the law redrawn (shorter reference period + 14%); the family paid on terms the Council had forced upward. Not dropped — decided, in the owners' favor, at the constitutional level.

"*Prosecution of Baron Louis was dropped when he 'voluntarily' handed over $10,000,000 of his private fortune to the Government and resigned as the bank's president*" (TIME, 1939). The historical dropped case, priced.
